### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following pictorial diagram most correctly represents the $\pi^*$ ($\pi$-antibonding) molecular orbital between two atoms if the internuclear axis is taken to be in the z-direction ($\xrightarrow{\text{z-axis}}$)?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q53_d1.jpg" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q53_d2.jpg" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q53_d3.jpg" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q53_d4.jpg" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q53_d3.jpg" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

To determine the correct pictorial representation of a $\pi^{*}$ ($\pi$-antibonding) molecular orbital, we need to consider how it is formed.

1. A $\pi$ molecular orbital is formed by the sideways (lateral) overlap of atomic orbitals (like p-orbitals) whose axes are perpendicular to the internuclear axis (z-axis).

2. An antibonding molecular orbital ($\pi^{*}$) is formed by the out-of-phase (subtractive) overlap of these atomic orbitals. This means the positive lobe of one orbital overlaps with the negative lobe of the other.

3. Visually, this out-of-phase overlap results in a nodal plane between the two nuclei, perpendicular to the internuclear axis, where the electron density is zero. The lobes of the orbitals are often depicted as bending away from each other due to repulsion.

Evaluating the given options:
- Option (A) shows continuous electron density above and below the internuclear axis, which represents a $\pi$ bonding molecular orbital.
- Option (B) shows two p-orbitals with in-phase alignment (shaded lobe next to shaded lobe), which would combine to form a $\pi$ bonding orbital.
- Option (C) shows two p-orbitals with out-of-phase alignment (shaded lobe next to unshaded lobe). This creates a nodal plane between the nuclei, correctly representing a $\pi^{*}$ antibonding molecular orbital.
- Option (D) shows overlap along the internuclear axis with a node between the nuclei, which represents a $\sigma^{*}$ antibonding molecular orbital.

Therefore, the diagram in (C) most correctly represents the $\pi^{*}$ molecular orbital.

Answer: <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q53_d3.jpg" />

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
<b>Statement (I) :</b> The correct sequence of bond lengths in the following species is :
$O_2^+ < O_2 < O_2^- < O_2^{2-}$
<b>Statement (II) :</b> The correct sequence of number of unpaired electrons in the following species is :
$O_2 > O_2^+ > O_2^- > O_2^{2-}$
In the light of the above statements, choose the <b>correct answer</b> from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement I is true but Statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

The bond order of the given oxygen species can be calculated using molecular orbital theory:

For $O_2$ (16 electrons), the configuration is $\sigma_{1s}^2 \sigma^{*}_{1s}^2 \sigma_{2s}^2 \sigma^{*}_{2s}^2 \sigma_{2p_z}^2 \pi_{2p_x}^2 = \pi_{2p_y}^2 \pi^{*}_{2p_x}^1 = \pi^{*}_{2p_y}^1$.
Bond order = $\dfrac{10 - 6}{2} = 2$. Number of unpaired electrons = $2$.

For $O_2^+$ (15 electrons), one electron is removed from a $\pi^{*}$ orbital.
Bond order = $\dfrac{10 - 5}{2} = 2.5$. Number of unpaired electrons = $1$.

For $O_2^-$ (17 electrons), one electron is added to a $\pi^{*}$ orbital.
Bond order = $\dfrac{10 - 7}{2} = 1.5$. Number of unpaired electrons = $1$.

For $O_2^{2-}$ (18 electrons), two electrons are added to the $\pi^{*}$ orbitals.
Bond order = $\dfrac{10 - 8}{2} = 1$. Number of unpaired electrons = $0$.

Since bond length is inversely proportional to bond order, the correct sequence of bond lengths is $O_2^+ < O_2 < O_2^- < O_2^{2-}$. Thus, Statement I is true.

The number of unpaired electrons for the species are $2, 1, 1,$ and $0$ respectively. The correct sequence is $O_2 > O_2^+ = O_2^- > O_2^{2-}$. Thus, Statement II is false.

Answer: Statement I is true but Statement II is false

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: The number of species among $\mathrm{BF}_{4}{ }^{-}, \mathrm{SiF}_{4}, \mathrm{XeF}_{4}$ and $\mathrm{SF}_{4}$, that have unequal $\mathrm{E}-\mathrm{F}$ bond lengths is two. Here, E is the central atom. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: Among $\mathrm{O}_{2}^{-}, \mathrm{O}_{2}{ }^{2-}, \mathrm{F}_{2}$ and $\mathrm{O}_{2}{ }^{+}, \mathrm{O}_{2}^{-}$has the highest bond order. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(B)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are false**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: Let's analyze the geometry of the given species.
$BF_4^-$: $sp^3$ hybridization, tetrahedral geometry. All B-F bond lengths are equal.
$SiF_4$: $sp^3$ hybridization, tetrahedral geometry. All Si-F bond lengths are equal.
$XeF_4$: $sp^3d^2$ hybridization, square planar geometry. All Xe-F bond lengths are equal.
$SF_4$: $sp^3d$ hybridization, see-saw geometry. It has axial and equatorial bonds. Axial bonds are longer than equatorial bonds due to greater repulsion. Thus, only $SF_4$ has unequal bond lengths. The number of species is one, not two. Statement I is false.
Statement II: Bond order calculation using Molecular Orbital Theory:
Total electrons in $O_2^-$ = 17, Bond Order = 1.5
Total electrons in $O_2^{2-}$ = 18, Bond Order = 1.0
Total electrons in $F_2$ = 18, Bond Order = 1.0
Total electrons in $O_2^+$ = 15, Bond Order = 2.5
Among these, $O_2^+$ has the highest bond order (2.5), not $O_2^-$. Statement II is false.
Both Statement I and Statement II are false.

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Pair of species among the following having same bond order as well as paramagnetic character will be-

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{O}_{2}{ }^{-}, \mathrm{N}_{2}{ }^{+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{O}_{2}{ }^{+}, \mathrm{N}_{2}{ }^{2-}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{O}_{2}{ }^{-}, \mathrm{N}_{2}{ }^{-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{O}_{2}{ }^{+}, \mathrm{N}_{2}{ }^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{O}_{2}{ }^{+}, \mathrm{N}_{2}{ }^{-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Bond orders and magnetic character:
O₂⁻ (BO=1.5, paramagnetic), O₂⁺ (BO=2.5, paramagnetic), O₂²⁻ (BO=1, diamagnetic);
N₂⁺ (BO=2.5, paramagnetic), N₂⁻ (BO=2.5, paramagnetic), N₂²⁻ (BO=2, diamagnetic)
<b>1</b>: BO 1.5 vs 2.5 (different);
<b>2</b>: BO 2.5 vs 2 (different);
<b>3</b>: BO 1.5 vs 2.5 (different);
<b>4</b>: O₂⁺ (BO=2.5, paramagnetic) and N₂⁻ (BO=2.5, paramagnetic) - both same bond order and paramagnetic character

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2025 (4 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following molecules(s) show/s paramagnetic behavior?
(A) $\mathrm{O}_2$
(B) $\mathrm{N}_2$
(C) $\mathrm{F}_2$
(D) $\mathrm{S}_2$
(E) $\mathrm{Cl}_2$
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** B only
- **(B)** A & C only
- **(C)** A & E only
- **(D)** A & D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A & D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{lcc} 
& & \text { No. of unpaired } \mathrm{e}^{-} \\
\text {(A) } & \mathrm{O}_2 & 2 \\
\text { (B) } & \mathrm{N}_2 & 0 \\
\text { (C) } & \mathrm{F}_2 & 0 \\
\text { (D) } & \mathrm{S}_2 & 2 \\
\text { (E) } & \mathrm{Cl}_2 & 0
\end{array}$
If species contain unpaired electron than it is paramagnetic.
So A \& D are paramagnetic.

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Total number of molecules/species from following which will be paramagnetic is ______ 
$\mathrm{O}_2, \mathrm{O}_2^{+}, \mathrm{O}_2^{-}, \mathrm{NO}, \mathrm{NO}_2, \mathrm{CO}, \mathrm{K}_2\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right],\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right] \mathrm{Cl}_3, \mathrm{~K}_2\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_4\right]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{O}_2 \rightarrow 2$ unpaired electrons according to MOT
$\mathrm{O}_2^{+} \rightarrow 1$ unpaired electrons according to MOT
$\mathrm{O}_2^{-} \rightarrow 1$ unpaired electrons according to MOT
$\mathrm{NO} \rightarrow$ odd electron species
$\mathrm{NO}_2 \rightarrow$ odd electron species
$\mathrm{K}_2\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right] \rightarrow \mathrm{Ni}^{2+} \Rightarrow 3 \mathrm{~d}^8$ weak Ligand, C.N. $=4$
$\Rightarrow$ Tetrahedral, Paramagnetic with 2 unpaired electrons

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Total number of electrons present in \(\left(\pi^*\right)\) molecular orbitals of \(\mathrm{O}_2, \mathrm{O}_2{ }^{+}\) and \(\mathrm{O}_2^{-}\) is _______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{O}_2(16 \mathrm{e}):\left(\sigma_{1 \mathrm{~s}}\right)^2\left(\sigma_{1 \mathrm{~s}}^*\right)^2\left(\sigma_{2 \mathrm{~s}}\right)^2\left(\sigma_{2 \mathrm{~s}}^*\right)^2 \\
& \left(\sigma_{2 \mathrm{p}}\right)^2\left[\left(\pi_{2 \mathrm{p}}\right)^2=\left(\pi_{2 \mathrm{p}}\right)^2\right],\left[\left(\pi_{2 \mathrm{p}}^*\right)^1=\left(\mathrm{m}_{2 \mathrm{p}}^*\right)^1\right]
\end{aligned}$
Number of $\mathrm{e}^{-}$present in $\left(\pi^*\right)$ of $\mathrm{O}_2=2$
Number of $\mathrm{e}^{-}$present in $\left(\pi^*\right)$ of $\mathrm{O}_2^{+}=1$
Number of $\mathrm{e}^{-}$present in $\left(\pi^*\right)$ of $\mathrm{O}_2^{-}=3$
So total $\mathrm{e}^{-}$in $\left(\pi^*\right)=2+1+3=6$

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Number of molecules having bond order 2 from the following molecules is ________ \(\mathrm{C}_2, \mathrm{O}_2, \mathrm{Be}_2, \mathrm{Li}_2, \mathrm{Ne}_2, \mathrm{~N}_2, \mathrm{He}_2\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{C}_2 \\ & \left(12 \mathrm{e}^{-}\right): \sigma 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma 2 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 2 \mathrm{~s}^2\left[\pi 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{x}}^2=\pi 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{y}}^2\right] \\ & \text { B.O. }=\frac{8-4}{2}=2 \\ & \mathrm{O}_2 \\ & \left(16 \mathrm{e}^{-}\right): \sigma 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma 2 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 2 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma 2 \mathrm{pz}^2 \\ & {\left[\pi 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{x}}^2=\pi 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{y}}^2\right]\left[\pi * 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{x}}^1=\pi * 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{y}}^1\right]} \\ & \text { B.O. }=\frac{10-6}{2}=2 \\ & \text { Be }{ }_2 \\ & \left(8 \mathrm{e}^{-}\right): \sigma 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma 2 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 2 \mathrm{~s}^2 \\ & \text { B.O. }=\frac{4-4}{2}=0 \\ & \mathrm{Li}_2 \\ & \left(6 \mathrm{e}^{-}\right): \sigma 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma 2 \mathrm{~s}^2\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \text { B.O. }=\frac{4-2}{2}=1 \\ & \mathrm{Ne}_2 \\ & \left(20 \mathrm{e}^{-}\right): \sigma 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma 2 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 2 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma 2 \mathrm{pz}^2 \\ & {\left[\pi 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{x}}^2=\pi 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{y}}^2\right]\left[\pi * 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{x}}^2=\pi^* 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{y}}^2\right] \sigma^* 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{z}}^2} \\ & \text { B.O. }=\frac{10-10}{2}=0 \\ & \mathrm{~N}_2 \\ & \left(14 \mathrm{e}^{-}\right): \sigma 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma 2 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 2 \mathrm{~s}^2\left[\pi 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{x}}^2=\pi 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{y}}^2\right] \sigma 2 \mathrm{p}_{\mathrm{z}}^2 \\ & \text { B.O. }=\frac{10-4}{2}=6 \\ & \mathrm{He}_2 \\ & \left(4 \mathrm{e}^{-}\right): \sigma 1 \mathrm{~s}^2, \sigma^* 1 \mathrm{~s}^2 \\ & \text { B.O. }=\frac{2-2}{2}=0\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

When \(\psi_{\mathrm{A}}\) and \(\psi_{\mathrm{B}}\) are the wave functions of atomic orbitals, then \(\sigma^*\) is represented by :

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\psi_{\mathrm{A}}+2 \psi_{\mathrm{B}}\)
- **(B)** \(\psi_A-\psi_B\)
- **(C)** \(\psi_{\mathrm{A}}+\psi_{\mathrm{B}}\)
- **(D)** \(\psi_A-2 \psi_B\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) \(\psi_A-\psi_B\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Antibonding molecular orbitals are formed by destructive interference of wave functions.
$(\mathrm{ABMO}) \sigma^*=\psi_{\mathrm{A}}-\psi_{\mathrm{B}}$

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements :
Statement (I) : A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></math> bonding <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MO</mi></math> has lower electron density above and below the inter-nuclear axis.</p><p>Statement (II) : The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>*</mo></msup></math> antibonding <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MO</mi></math> has a node between the nuclei.</p><p>In light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement I is false but Statement II is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

The electron density is minimum along the internuclear axis due to the nodal plane.</p><p>A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></math> bonding molecular orbital has higher electron density above and below inter nuclear axis.</p><p>This <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>*</mo></msup></math> orbital has two nodes: one node is the plane which contains the atoms, and the other node is a plane perpendicular to this, between the two carbon atoms.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/afb1eb93-f506-4bd6-ad1e-51d3e6aea356.png" style="width: 602px; height: 404px;"/></p>

### Q14. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The linear combination of atomic orbitals to form molecular orbitals takes place only when the combining atomic orbitals</p><p>A. have the same energy  B. have the minimum overlap  C. have same symmetry about the molecular axis  D. have different symmetry about the molecular axis</p><p>Choose the most appropriate from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, B, C only
- **(B)** A and C only
- **(C)** B, C, D only
- **(D)** B and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A and C only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The given conditions should be satisfied by atomic orbitals to form molecular orbitals:</p><p>(a) The atomic orbital with one electron (completely half filled) can take part in combination or overlapping.</p><p>(b) During the time of overlap, the combining orbitals must have opposite spins of electrons.</p><p>(c) The combining atomic orbitals must have the same or nearly the same energy. This means that in a homonuclear molecule, the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>s-atomic orbital of an atom can combine with the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>s-atomic orbital of another atom, and not with the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>s-orbital.</p><p>(d) The combining atomic orbitals must have proper orientations to ensure that the overlap is maximum.</p><p>(e) The extent of overlapping should be large.</p><p>The combining atoms should have the same symmetry around the molecular axis for proper combination, otherwise, the electron density will be sparse.</p><p>Hence option B is the answer.</p>

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The total number of molecular orbitals formed from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> atomic orbitals of a diatomic molecule

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **08**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>2s and 2p atomic orbitals combine, forming σ (sigma) and π (pi) molecular orbitals. σ orbitals result from head-on overlap, while π orbitals arise from sideways overlap. This molecular orbital theory explains the bonding in molecules</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn></mn></math><mo> </mo><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> orbital will form, two molecular orbitals <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> orbital will form six molecular orbitals <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></msub></math>. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></math></p><p>Hence the total number of molecular orbitals formed from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> atomic orbitals of a diatomic molecule formed is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The total number of anti bonding molecular orbitals, formed from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>s</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>p</mi></math> atomic orbitals in a diatomic molecule is _____________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The atomic orbitals combine to form a new set of equivalent orbitals known as hybrid orbitals. Unlike pure orbitals, hybrid orbitals are used in bond formation. The phenomenon is known as hybridisation, which can be defined as the process of intermixing of the orbitals of slightly different energies to redistribute their energies, resulting in the formation of a new set of orbitals of equivalent energies and shape.</p><p>The number of hybrid orbitals is equal to the number of atomic orbitals that get hybridised.</p><p>Antibonding molecular orbital from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> that is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p><p>Antibonding molecular orbital from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> that is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><mo> </mo><mi>and</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></msub></math>.</p><p>Total <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q17. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The number of species from the following which are paramagnetic and with bond order equal to one is
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>He</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>Ne</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Paramagnetic molecules are those molecules in which unpaired electrons are present, while diamagnetic molecules are those in which unpaired electrons are absent.</p><p>The diatomic species with an odd number of electrons and those diatomic species that contain 10 or 16 electrons are paramagnetic. Among the given species <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>(</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo></math> are paramgnetic species.
The molecular orbital electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">σ</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>1</mn></msubsup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>1</mn></msubsup></math></p><p>Bond order<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>Bonding</mi><mo> </mo><mi>electrons</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>antibonding</mi><mo> </mo><mi>electrons</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Hence. the answer is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q18. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Sum of bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Bond order is the number of chemical bonds between a pair of atoms and indicates the stability of a bond. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Bond</mi><mo> </mo><mi>order</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>Number</mi><mo> </mo><mi>of</mi><mo> </mo><mi>bonding</mi><mo> </mo><mi>electrons</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>number</mi><mo> </mo><mi>of</mi><mo> </mo><mi>antibonding</mi><mo> </mo><mi>electrons</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>The given two molecules have the same number of electrons(14) like nitrogen molecule.</p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi><mo>⇒</mo><mo>  </mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>≡</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo></mover><mo>  </mo><mo>:</mo><mi>BO</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>≡</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>  </mo><mo>:</mo><mi>BO</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>The sum of the bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi><mo> </mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2023 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The bond order and magnetic property of acetylide ion are same as that of

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo></msup></math>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The bond order of the acetylide ion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HC</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>, indicating a triple bond between the carbon atoms.</p><p>The acetylide ion consists of two carbon atoms with a triple bond between them and a negative charge. In the triple bond, there are six bonding electrons (two pi bonds and one sigma bond) and zero antibonding electrons.</p><p>Among the given options <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> will have the same bond order and magnetic property like acetylide ion.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" Molecular orbital configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KK</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>*</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#120587;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#120587;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></msub><msup><mo>)</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Bond order = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msub><mo>)</mo></math> = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>So, according to molecular orbital theory, the bond orders respectively are 1, 3 and 3.</p>

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The number of molecules or ions from the following, which do not have odd number of electrons are _____ .
(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>ICl</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>
(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BrF</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ClO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
(E) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math>
(F) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>ICl</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>BrF</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math> do not have odd number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> as we can see from their lewis structures respectively:
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/77dbd89c-82ae-4d8b-a1bb-31c7044d459e-11.PNG" style="width: 160px; height: 134px;" />, <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1c1f3f3a-837c-4a83-b2e1-5eaf9009aa72-12.PNG" style="width: 160px; height: 96px;" /> and<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5becd639-b7d1-4b8c-beba-f33101108629-13.PNG" style="width: 160px; height: 52px;" /></p><p>The number of electrons in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>23</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>15</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ClO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>33</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> are odd.</p>

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The orbital in which last electron is present is called the highest occupied molecular orbital(HOMO).</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mi>HOMO</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi ### Q24. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The number of paramagnetic species among the following is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>Li</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>He</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The species that contain unpaired electrons are paramagnetic.</p><p>Electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Li</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> molecule is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#963;</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>&#963;</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#963;</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p>The electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> molecule is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>&#8902;</mo></msup><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>&#8902;</mo></msup><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>The presence of unpaired electrons in the two <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi></math> bonding molecular orbitals explains its paramagnetic nature.</p><p>The electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KK</mi><mi>&#963;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>&#963;</mi><mo>*</mo></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>p</mi><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>p</mi><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></msub></mrow></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KK</mi><msup><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></msub></mrow></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub></mrow></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></mrow></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msub><mo>*</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msub><mo>*</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence, the above species is paramagnetic.</p></blockquote>

### Q25. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Match List-I with List-II</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><strong>List-I</strong></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><strong>List-II</strong></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi>MO</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></math></td>			<td>I</td>			<td>Dipole moment</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math></td>			<td>II</td>			<td>Bonding molecular orbital</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></td>			<td>III</td>			<td>Anti-bonding molecualr orbital</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi>MO</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></math></td>			<td>IV</td>			<td>Bond order</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The molecular orbital &sigma; formed by the addition of atomic orbitals is called the bonding molecular orbital while the molecular orbital &sigma;* formed by the subtraction of atomic orbital is called antibonding molecular orbital.</p><p>The electron density in a bonding molecular orbital is located between the nuclei of the bonded atoms because of which the repulsion between the nuclei is very less while in case of an antibonding molecular orbital, most of the electron density is located away from the space between the nuclei. Infact, there is a nodal plane (on which the electron density is zero) between the nuclei and hence the repulsion between the nuclei is high.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><strong>List-I</strong></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><strong>List-II</strong></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi>MO</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></math></td>			<td>I</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ABMO</mi></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math></td>			<td>II</td>			<td>Dipole moment</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></td>			<td>III</td>			<td>Bond order</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi>MO</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#936;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></math></td>			<td>IV</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BMO</mi></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table></blockquote>

</details>

### Q26. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>According to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MO</mi></math> theory, number of species/ions from the following having identical bond order is____</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The bond order of a diatomic species can be calculated using molecular orbital theory as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Bond</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>order</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>No</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BMO</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>electrons</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>No</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ABMO</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>electrons</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>BMO = bonding molecular orbital</p><p>ABMO = Anti bonding molecular orbital.</p><p>Based on the total number electrons in bond orders are given in the following table.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 200px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;">Number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Bond order</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>13</mn></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn></math></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> are having 14 electrons each, hence the bond order is 3.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Amongst the following the number of oxide(s) which are paramagnetic in nature is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>KO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>ClO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>NO</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The magnetic properties of a substance can be determined by examining its electron configuration: If it has unpaired electrons, then the substance is paramagnetic and if all electrons are paired, the substance is then diamagnetic.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is potassium superoxide. There is one unpaired electron in anti-bonding molecular orbital of superoxide. Potassium superoxide is paramagnetic due to the presence of unpaired electron.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>ClO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>NO</mi></math> are odd electron species. Hence, they are paramagnetic.</p><p>The other are monoxide and dioxide contain paired electrons, so they will show diamagnetic behaviour.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements.</p><p>Statement I : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> are weakly attracted by magnetic field and are magnetized in the same direction as magnetic field.</p><p>Statement II : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> are weakly magnetized in opposite direction to magnetic field.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer form the options given below</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct.
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect.
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect.
- **(D)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> all have unpaired electrons so, they are paramagnetic,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>has</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>two</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>unpaired</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>electrons</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>according</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>to</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>MOT</mi><mo>,</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>has</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>9</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>configuration</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>so</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>one</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>unpaired</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>electron</mi><mo>.</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>has</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>configuration</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>so</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>unpaired</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>electrons</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Weakly attracted by magnetic field.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> does not have any unpaired electrons so they are diamagnetic,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Weakly repelled by magnetic field.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Consider the ions/molecule <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>. For increasing bond order the correct option is

### Q31. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Bonding in which of the following diatomic molecule(s) become(s) stronger, on the basis of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MO</mi></math> Theory, by removal of an electron?</p><p>(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math></p><p>(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>(E) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>Choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> only
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mfenced></math> only
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> only
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced></math> only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math> only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p style="text-align: justify;">In all these molecules <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8203;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NO</mi></math>, is stabilized by removal of electrons because it is having electrons in the anti-bonding orbital. And thus removal of electron from the anti bonding orbital, increases the bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NO</mi></math>​. And thus is becomes more stable after removal of electrons. Bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>&#8203;</mo></math>is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> and it is more than that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>​ which is having bond order as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>. Similarly Bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>is</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>that</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>NO</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>is</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct order of bond orders of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Bond order <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math> Number of electrons in bonding molecular orbitals.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo></math> Number of electrons in anti bonding molecular orbitals.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn></math> electrons, it is isoelectronic with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> where Bond order <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn></math> electrons is isoelectronic with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> whose bond order <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn></math> electrons and is isoelectronic with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> whose bond order <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>So, the correct order of Bond order is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The spin-only magnetic moment value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math> species is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>BM</mi></math> (Nearest integer)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mtext>&#160;Given :&#160;</mtext><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>]</mo></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **173**

**Detailed Solution:**

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#62;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math>

Bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

### Q40. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AX</mi></math> is a covalent diatomic molecule where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> are second row elements of periodic table. Based on Molecular orbital theory, the bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AX</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo></math> The total number of electrons in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AX</mi></math> is_____. (Round off to the Nearest Integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **15**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Bond order of the covalent molecule, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AX</mi></math> is given as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math>, As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AX</mi></math>  is a covalent diatomic molecule of second row elements of periodic table we can consider <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NO</mi></math>  but bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> having <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn></math>  electrons which is isoelectronic to nitrogen should be equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math>  having 15 electrons will have a bond order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math>.</p><p>Molecular orbital configuration and bond order calculation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math> is</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d7d1e62a-d045-4801-9f02-0b74812195cc-MOOFNO.png" style="width: 711px; height: 128px;" /></p><p>ABMO<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math> antibonding molecular orbital</p><p>Bond order <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math>Number of bonding electrons <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo></math> number of anti bonding electrons.</p><p>Note: Total number of electrons equal to 13 will also have the 2.5 bond order. But in this case neutral diatomic molecule will not be possible</p></blockquote>

</details>

<p>Match List-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> with List-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math>.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1">List-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math></td>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1">List-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1">(Molecule)</td>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1">(Bond order)</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(a)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Ne</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></td>			<td>(i)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(b)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></td>			<td>(ii)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(c)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></td>			<td>(iii)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(d)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></td>			<td>(iv)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math>

### Q42. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

According to molecular orbital theory, the species among the following that does not exist is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>He</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>He</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Be</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Be</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td>Chemical Species</td>			<td>Bond Order</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>He</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>He</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Be</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>According to M.O.T. If bond order of chemical species is zero then that chemical species does not exist.</p><p>The bond order (half the difference in the numbers of bonding and antibonding electrons) for such a diatomic entity is zero. </p>

</details>

### Q43. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The intermolecular potential energy for the molecules<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> ,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> , <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math> given below suggests that:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ff423fa9-a8af-4f27-ae0f-0124c7f2324d-image.png" style="width: 299px; height: 220px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math> has the shortest bond length
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> has the largest bond enthalpy
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math> is more electronegative than other atoms.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> has the stiffest bond.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> has the stiffest bond.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Bond enthalpy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math> bond is highest so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> bond is more strong and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is highest electronegative atom. Order of bond length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

If the magnetic moment of a di-oxygen species is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1.73&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math>, it may be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> or <math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>.
- **(B)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> or <math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>.
- **(C)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> or <math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>.
- **(D)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math> or <math><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

<p>Given, magnetic moment of di-oxygen species<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>We know, magnetic moment <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><msqrt> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext><mfenced> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo></math>number of unpaired electrons

<math> <mrow> <mn>1.73</mn><mo>=</mo><msqrt> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext><mfenced> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </math>
