### Q67. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which one of the following properties is <strong>not</strong> shown by NO ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** It is diamagnetic in gaseous state
- **(B)** It is a neutral oxide
- **(C)** It combines with oxygen to form nitrogen dioxide
- **(D)** It&#39;s bond order is 2.5

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) It is diamagnetic in gaseous state**

**Detailed Solution:**

As per MOT, electronic configuration of NO is

<math> <mrow> <mi>σ</mi><mn>1</mn><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>&lt;</mo><mover> <mi>σ</mi> <mo>*</mo> </mover> <mn>1</mn><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>&lt;</mo><mi>σ</mi><mn>2</mn><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>&lt;</mo><mover> <mi>σ</mi> <mo>*</mo> </mover> <mn>2</mn><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>&lt;</mo><mi>σ</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup> <mi>p</mi> <mi>z</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> <mo>&lt;</mo><mi>π</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup> <mi>p</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo><mi>π</mi><mn>2</mn><msubsup> <mi>p</mi> <mi>y</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> <mo>&lt;</mo><mover> <mi>π</mi> <mo>*</mo> </mover> <mn>2</mn><msubsup> <mi>p</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo><mover> <mi>π</mi> <mo>*</mo> </mover> <mn>2</mn><msubsup> <mi>p</mi> <mi>y</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>

It is paramagnetic due to <math> <mrow> <mi>π</mi> <msup> <mn>2</mn> <mo>*</mo> </msup> <msup> <mtext>py</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math> (unpaired electron) 

Bond order of NO <math> <mrow> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>i</mtext><mo>.</mo><mtext>e</mtext><mo>.</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><msubsup> <mtext>O</mtext> <mrow> <msub> <mrow></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mo>+</mo> </msubsup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>10</mn><mo>−</mo><mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mn>2.5</mn> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The internuclear distances in $\mathrm{O}-\mathrm{O}$ bonds for $\mathrm{O}_2^{+}, \mathrm{O}_2, \mathrm{O}_2^{-}$and $\mathrm{O}_2^{2-}$ respectively are :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.30 ~Å, 1.49 ~Å, 1.12 ~Å, 1.21 ~Å$
- **(B)** $1.49 ~Å, 1.21 ~Å, 1.12 ~Å, 1.30 ~Å$
- **(C)** $1.21 ~Å, 1.12 ~Å, 1.49 ~Å, 1.30 ~Å$
- **(D)** $1.12 ~Å, 1.21 ~Å, 1.30 ~Å, 1.49 ~Å$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.12 ~Å, 1.21 ~Å, 1.30 ~Å, 1.49 ~Å$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The bond length follows the order
$\mathrm{O}_2^{+} < \mathrm{O}_2 < \mathrm{O}_2^{-} < \mathrm{O}_2^{2-}$
According to this the possible values are 
$1.12 ~Å, 1.21 ~Å, 1.30 ~Å, 1.49 ~Å$

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Bond order normally gives idea of stability of a molecular species. All the molecules viz. $\mathrm{H}_2, \mathrm{Li}_2$ and $\mathrm{B}_2$ have the same bond order yet they are not equally stable. Their stability order is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{H}_2>\mathrm{B}_2>\mathrm{Li}_2$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Li}_2>\mathrm{H}_2>\mathrm{B}_2$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Li}_2>\mathrm{B}_2>\mathrm{H}_2$
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) None of these**

**Detailed Solution:**

The molecular orbital configuration of the given molecules is
$\mathrm{H}_2=\sigma 1 s^2$ (no electron anti-bonding)
$\mathrm{Li}_2=\sigma 1 s^2 \sigma^* 1 s^2 \sigma 2 s^2$ (two anti-bonding electrons)
$$
\mathrm{B}_2=\sigma 1 s^2 \sigma^* 1 s^2 \sigma 2 s^2 \sigma^* 2 s^2
$$
$$
\left\{\pi 2 p_y^1=\pi 2 p_z^1\right\}
$$
(4 anti-bonding electrons)
Though the bond order of all the species are same $(\mathrm{B} . \mathrm{O}=1)$ but stability is different. This is due to difference in the presence of no. of anti-bonding electron.
Higher the no. of anti-bonding electron lower is the stability hence the correct order is
$$
\mathrm{H}_2>\mathrm{Li}_2>\mathrm{B}_2
$$

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In which of the following ionization processes the bond energy has increased and also the magnetic behaviour has changed from paramagnetic to diamagnetic ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{NO} \rightarrow \mathrm{NO}^{+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{N}_2 \rightarrow \mathrm{N}_2^{+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{C}_2 \rightarrow \mathrm{C}_2^{+}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{O}_2 \rightarrow \mathrm{O}_2^{+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{NO} \rightarrow \mathrm{NO}^{+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For $\mathrm{NO}$
Total no. of electrons $=15$ 
$\mathrm{B.O}=2.5$
Mag. Behaviour $=$ Paramagnetic 
For $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$
Total no. of electrons $=14$ 
$\mathrm{B.O}$=3$
Mag. Behaviour $=$ Diamagnetic

</details>

**Detailed Solution:**

<strong>(i)</strong>   <math> <mrow> <mtable> <mtr> <mtd> <msubsup> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msubsup> </mtd> <mtd> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>B</mtext> <mtext>.</mtext> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <msub> <mtext>He</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mtd> <mtd> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>B</mtext> <mtext>.</mtext> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> <mo>}</mo> </mrow> </math>

<strong>(ii) </strong>  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><msubsup><mtext>H</mtext><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo></mtd><mtd><mtext>B</mtext><mtext>.</mtext><mtext>O</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mtext>.</mtext><mn>5</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msubsup><mtext>He</mtext><mn>2</mn><mn>2+</mn></msubsup></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo></mtd><mtd><mtext>B</mtext><mtext>.</mtext><mtext>O</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

<strong>(iii)</strong>   <math> <mrow> <mtable> <mtr> <mtd> <msubsup> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> </msubsup> </mtd> <mtd> <mo>=</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>B</mtext> <mtext>.</mtext> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>5</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <msubsup> <mtext>He</mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>-</mo> </mrow> </msubsup> </mtd> <mtd> <mo>=</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>B</mtext> <mtext>.</mtext> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> </mrow> </math>

<strong>(iv)</strong>   <math> <mrow> <mtable> <mtr> <mtd> <msubsup> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mo>-</mo> </msubsup> </mtd> <mtd> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>B</mtext> <mtext>.</mtext> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>5</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <msubsup> <mtext>He</mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>-</mo> </mrow> </msubsup> </mtd> <mtd> <mo>&#8658;</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mtext>B</mtext> <mtext>.</mtext> <mtext>O</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> </mrow> </math>


As both H<sub>2</sub><sup>2+</sup> and He<sub>2</sub> have zero bond order and hence do not exist.

</details>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>Li</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>*</mo></mover><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.5</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>Li</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>Li</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math> both have same bond order, but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>Li</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math> has more number of electron in anti-bonding molecular orbitals (experience more inter electronic repulsion) and hence least stable among three species.

</details>

### Q74. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Among the following, the species having the smallest bond is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{NO}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{O}_2$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{NO}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{NO}^{-}(16)-$ B.O. $-2 \quad \mathrm{O}_2(16)-$ B.O. $-2$ $\mathrm{NO}^{+}(14)-$ B.O. $-3 \quad \mathrm{NO}(15)-$ B.O. $-2.5$ Higher the bond order lower is the bond length. Hence $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$will have smallest bond.

</details>

---

### Q75. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Using MO theory predict which of the following species has the shortest bond length ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{2+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{2-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{O}_2^{2+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Bond length $\alpha \frac{1}{\text { bond order }}$
Bond order $=\frac{\text { no..of bonding } \overline{\mathrm{e}}-\text { no.of antibonding } \overline{\mathrm{e}}}{2}$
Bond orders of $\mathrm{O}_2^{+}, \mathrm{O}_2^{-}, \mathrm{O}_2^{-2}$ and $\mathrm{O}_2^{+2}$ are respectively $2.5,1.5,1$ and 3 .

</details>

Which one of the following pairs of species have the same bond order?

- **(A)** $\mathrm{CN}^{-}$and $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{CN}^{-}$and $\mathrm{CN}^{+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{-}$and $\mathrm{CN}^{-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$and $\mathrm{CN}^{+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{CN}^{-}$and $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$**

Both are isoelectronic and have same bond order.

### Q77. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which of the following species exhibits the diamagnetic behaviour?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{2-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{O}_2$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{NO}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{O}_2^{2-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The correct option is $\mathrm{O}_2^{2-}$
This species has $18 \mathrm{~e}^{-}$, which are filled in such a way that all molecular orbitals are fully filled, so diamagnetic.
$\mathrm{\sigma 1 s^2 \sigma^* 1 s^2, \sigma 2 s^2 \sigma^* 2 s^2, \sigma 2 p_z^2, \pi 2 p_x^2=\pi 2 p_y^2, \pi^* 2 p_x^2=\pi^* 2 p_y^2}$
Hence, (A) is correct.

</details>

---

### Q78. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In which of the following ionization processes, the bond order has increased and the magnetic behaviour has changed?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{C}_2 \longrightarrow \mathrm{C}_2^{+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{NO} \longrightarrow \mathrm{NO}^{+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{O}_2 \longrightarrow \mathrm{O}_2^{+}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{N}_2 \longrightarrow \mathrm{N}_2^{+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{NO} \longrightarrow \mathrm{NO}^{+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In $\mathrm{C}_2-\mathrm{C}_2{ }^{+}$electron is removed from bonding molecular orbital so bond order decreases. In $\mathrm{NO}$ $\longrightarrow \mathrm{NO}^{+}$, electron is removed from anti bonding molecular orbital so bond order increases and nature changes from paramagnetic to diamagnetic. 
Hence, (B) is correct.

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The bond order in $\mathrm{NO}$ is $2.5$ while that in $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$is 3 . Which of the following statements is true for these two species?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Bond length in $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$is greater than in $\mathrm{NO}$
- **(B)** Bond length is unpredictable
- **(C)** Bond length in $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$in equal to that in $\mathrm{NO}$
- **(D)** Bond length in $\mathrm{NO}$ is greater than in $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Bond length in $\mathrm{NO}$ is greater than in $\mathrm{NO}^{+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Bond order of $\mathrm{NO}=2.5$
Bond order of $\mathrm{NO}^{+}=3$
Higher the bond order shorter is the bond length

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following are arranged in an increasing order of their bond strengths ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{-} < \mathrm{O}_2 < \mathrm{O}_2^{+} < \mathrm{O}_2^{2-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{2-} < \mathrm{O}_2^{-} < \mathrm{O}_2 < \mathrm{O}_2^{+}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{-} < \mathrm{O}_2^{2-} < \mathrm{O}_2 < \mathrm{O}_2^{+}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{O}_2^{+} < \mathrm{O}_2 < \mathrm{O}_2^{-} < \mathrm{O}_2^{2-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{O}_2^{2-} < \mathrm{O}_2^{-} < \mathrm{O}_2 < \mathrm{O}_2^{+}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to bond order values the given order is the answer. Bond order values are 
$+1,+1 \frac{1}{2},+2$ and $+2 \frac{1}{2}$, higher bond order means stronger bond.

</details>
